People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

Yeah, um… I came in because I haven’t really been eating much the last few weeks. Like, I’ll get off shift and usually I’ve got certain foods I look forward to, you know, something easy after standing around watching monitors all night—but lately it just kind of sits there. I’ll take a few bites and have to force it down.

It’s weird because I used to be the guy who could eat whatever after work, especially if the break room had leftovers, but now I just don’t feel hungry. That’s mainly why I figured I should come in.

I’ll look like I’m listening, but inside it’s just kind of dull.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant weight loss?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it significant exactly… I haven’t really been stepping on a scale. I force down a few bites and then I’m done.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Guilty? I mean… not really sure. I’ll look like I’m listening, but inside it’s just kind of dull.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant weight loss?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it significant exactly… I haven’t really been stepping on a scale. My pants feel a little looser, maybe, but the bigger thing is I just haven’t been eating much. Like after work, I’ll make something I normally like, and it just sits there while I pick at it. I force down a few bites and then I’m done.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Guilty? I mean… not really sure. I guess everybody feels a little bad about stuff, like not keeping up with things or snapping in traffic or whatever, but I don’t know if I’d call it guilt.\n\nSometimes at work, watching the monitors, my mind kind of drifts and then I think, “Man, I should be paying more attention,” but that’s not really… I don’t know if that’s what you mean. So, no, nothing big that I can point to.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're fat or overweight, even when others say you're not?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t really think that. I mean, I’m not in great shape or anything, I’ve got that middle-aged security guard diet sometimes, vending machine stuff and coffee, but I don’t sit there thinking I’m fat when people say I’m not.\n\nHonestly, I don’t think about my body like that much. My uniform either fits or it doesn’t, and that’s kind of where my mind goes.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything wrong with your appearance?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, I look tired sometimes, but who doesn’t, right? The lighting in the surveillance room is awful, kind of makes everybody look half-dead on those little reflective screens. Sometimes I’ll catch myself in the bathroom mirror at work and think, “Yeah, you could use a haircut,” or I’ve got stubble or whatever.\n\nBut as far as something wrong with how I look… no, not really. I don’t sit around thinking that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any episodes where you ate a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like, sitting down and eating some huge amount all at once. I haven’t really been checking. I mean, the main thing I noticed is just… I’ll get home after shift and make something I normally like, and it just sits there. I take a few bites and then I’m kind of done. It’s been like that the last few weeks.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in interest in activities you normally enjoy outside of eating?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so. I used to actually look forward to stuff like my morning runs, messing around on guitar, cooking dinner—nothing fancy, just kind of my routine. Lately I keep skipping them because they feel like chores, like I can’t get myself to care enough to start.\n\nI mean, the guitar’s just sitting there in the corner, and I’ll think, “I should pick that up,” then I end up doing nothing. Same with running—used to clear my head a bit, but now it’s like… eh, why bother.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, I think. It’s not always like crying sad or anything, it’s more this heavy, flat feeling I’m carrying around. Like I can be at home with my family, or sitting in the break room at work, and everybody’s talking, and I’m just… not really there.\n\nI don’t know, it’s hard to explain. I’ll look like I’m listening, but inside it’s just kind of dull.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant weight loss?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it significant exactly… I haven’t really been stepping on a scale. My pants feel a little looser, maybe, but the bigger thing is I just haven’t been eating much. Like after work, I’ll make something I normally like, and it just sits there while I pick at it. I force down a few bites and then I’m done.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Guilty? I mean… not really sure. I guess everybody feels a little bad about stuff, like not keeping up with things or snapping in traffic or whatever, but I don’t know if I’d call it guilt.\n\nSometimes at work, watching the monitors, my mind kind of drifts and then I think, “Man, I should be paying more attention,” but that’s not really… I don’t know if that’s what you mean. So, no, nothing big that I can point to.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're fat or overweight, even when others say you're not?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t really think that. I mean, I’m not in great shape or anything, I’ve got that middle-aged security guard diet sometimes, vending machine stuff and coffee, but I don’t sit there thinking I’m fat when people say I’m not.\n\nHonestly, I don’t think about my body like that much. My uniform either fits or it doesn’t, and that’s kind of where my mind goes.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything wrong with your appearance?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, I look tired sometimes, but who doesn’t, right? The lighting in the surveillance room is awful, kind of makes everybody look half-dead on those little reflective screens. Sometimes I’ll catch myself in the bathroom mirror at work and think, “Yeah, you could use a haircut,” or I’ve got stubble or whatever.\n\nBut as far as something wrong with how I look… no, not really. I don’t sit around thinking that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any episodes where you ate a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like, sitting down and eating some huge amount all at once. I mean, I’ll grab junk food on a shift sometimes, like chips or a sandwich from the machine, and then later realize I barely tasted it because I was watching the screens or checking doors.\n\nThere was this one guy I worked with who could demolish a whole pizza during break, and everyone would joke about it, but me… no, I don’t think I have episodes like that. At least nothing I’d call unusual.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten a large amount of food in a short period of time without being able to stop?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I’ve definitely eaten too fast before, especially on nights when you only get a few minutes and you’re trying to watch the cameras at the same time. You sort of inhale a sandwich and then realize you didn’t even enjoy it.\n\nBut not like I couldn’t stop myself. It’s more just bad shift habits, you know? Like vending machine stuff, whatever’s quick. I don’t remember ever feeling out of control with it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

I mean, my belt maybe feels a little looser, but I don’t know for sure. The bigger thing is I just haven’t been eating much these last few weeks. Stuff I usually like after a shift, I’ll heat it up and then it just kind of sits there, and I’m forcing down a few bites.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in how your clothes fit over the last few weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah, a little maybe—like my belt feels looser, but I’m not totally sure. I haven’t really been checking. I mean, the main thing I noticed is just… I’ll get home after shift and make something I normally like, and it just sits there. I take a few bites and then I’m kind of done. It’s been like that the last few weeks.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in interest in activities you normally enjoy outside of eating?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so. I used to actually look forward to stuff like my morning runs, messing around on guitar, cooking dinner—nothing fancy, just kind of my routine. Lately I keep skipping them because they feel like chores, like I can’t get myself to care enough to start.\n\nI mean, the guitar’s just sitting there in the corner, and I’ll think, “I should pick that up,” then I end up doing nothing. Same with running—used to clear my head a bit, but now it’s like… eh, why bother.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, I think. It’s not always like crying sad or anything, it’s more this heavy, flat feeling I’m carrying around. Like I can be at home with my family, or sitting in the break room at work, and everybody’s talking, and I’m just… not really there.\n\nI don’t know, it’s hard to explain. I’ll look like I’m listening, but inside it’s just kind of dull.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant weight loss?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it significant exactly… I haven’t really been stepping on a scale. I force down a few bites and then I’m done.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Guilty? I mean… not really sure. I guess everybody feels a little bad about stuff, like not keeping up with things or snapping in traffic or whatever, but I don’t know if I’d call it guilt.\n\nSometimes at work, watching the monitors, my mind kind of drifts and then I think, “Man, I should be paying more attention,” but that’s not really… I don’t know if that’s what you mean. Same with running—used to clear my head a bit, but now it’s like… eh, why bother.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, I think. It’s not always like crying sad or anything, it’s more this heavy, flat feeling I’m carrying around. I’ll look like I’m listening, but inside it’s just kind of dull.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant weight loss?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it significant exactly… I haven’t really been stepping on a scale. I force down a few bites and then I’m done.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Guilty? I mean… not really sure. I guess everybody feels a little bad about stuff, like not keeping up with things or snapping in traffic or whatever, but I don’t know if I’d call it guilt.\n\nSometimes at work, watching the monitors, my mind kind of drifts and then I think, “Man, I should be paying more attention,” but that’s not really… I don’t know if that’s what you mean. So, no, nothing big that I can point to.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're fat or overweight, even when others say you're not?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t really think that. My uniform either fits or it doesn’t, and that’s kind of where my mind goes.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything wrong with your appearance?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, I look tired sometimes, but who doesn’t, right? At least nothing I’d call unusual.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten a large amount of food in a short period of time without being able to stop?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I’ve definitely eaten too fast before, especially on nights when you only get a few minutes and you’re trying to watch the cameras at the same time. Like vending machine stuff, whatever’s quick. I don’t remember ever feeling out of control with it.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Afraid of gaining weight? I haven’t really been checking. I mean, the main thing I noticed is just… I’ll get home after shift and make something I normally like, and it just sits there. I take a few bites and then I’m kind of done. It’s been like that the last few weeks.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in interest in activities you normally enjoy outside of eating?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so. I used to actually look forward to stuff like my morning runs, messing around on guitar, cooking dinner—nothing fancy, just kind of my routine. Lately I keep skipping them because they feel like chores, like I can’t get myself to care enough to start.\n\nI mean, the guitar’s just sitting there in the corner, and I’ll think, “I should pick that up,” then I end up doing nothing. Same with running—used to clear my head a bit, but now it’s like… eh, why bother.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, I think. It’s not always like crying sad or anything, it’s more this heavy, flat feeling I’m carrying around. I’ll look like I’m listening, but inside it’s just kind of dull.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant weight loss?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it significant exactly… I haven’t really been stepping on a scale. I force down a few bites and then I’m done.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Guilty? I mean… not really sure. So, no, nothing big that I can point to.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're fat or overweight, even when others say you're not?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t really think that. My uniform either fits or it doesn’t, and that’s kind of where my mind goes.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything wrong with your appearance?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, I look tired sometimes, but who doesn’t, right? At least nothing I’d call unusual.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten a large amount of food in a short period of time without being able to stop?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I’ve definitely eaten too fast before, especially on nights when you only get a few minutes and you’re trying to watch the cameras at the same time. You sort of inhale a sandwich and then realize you didn’t even enjoy it.\n\nBut not like I couldn’t stop myself. It’s more just bad shift habits, you know? Like vending machine stuff, whatever’s quick. I don’t remember ever feeling out of control with it.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Afraid of gaining weight? I’ll take a few bites and have to force it down.\n\nIt’s weird because I used to be the guy who could eat whatever after work, especially if the break room had leftovers, but now I just don’t feel hungry. That’s mainly why I figured I should come in.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight over the last few weeks?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really checked the scale, honestly. I mean, my belt maybe feels a little looser, but I don’t know for sure. The bigger thing is I just haven’t been eating much these last few weeks. Stuff I usually like after a shift, I’ll heat it up and then it just kind of sits there, and I’m forcing down a few bites.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in how your clothes fit over the last few weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah, a little maybe—like my belt feels looser, but I’m not totally sure. I haven’t really been checking. I mean, the main thing I noticed is just… I’ll get home after shift and make something I normally like, and it just sits there. I take a few bites and then I’m kind of done. It’s been like that the last few weeks.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in interest in activities you normally enjoy outside of eating?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so. I used to actually look forward to stuff like my morning runs, messing around on guitar, cooking dinner—nothing fancy, just kind of my routine. Lately I keep skipping them because they feel like chores, like I can’t get myself to care enough to start.\n\nI mean, the guitar’s just sitting there in the corner, and I’ll think, “I should pick that up,” then I end up doing nothing. Same with running—used to clear my head a bit, but now it’s like… eh, why bother.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, I think. It’s not always like crying sad or anything, it’s more this heavy, flat feeling I’m carrying around. Like I can be at home with my family, or sitting in the break room at work, and everybody’s talking, and I’m just… not really there.\n\nI don’t know, it’s hard to explain. I’ll look like I’m listening, but inside it’s just kind of dull.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant weight loss?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it significant exactly… I haven’t really been stepping on a scale. My pants feel a little looser, maybe, but the bigger thing is I just haven’t been eating much. Like after work, I’ll make something I normally like, and it just sits there while I pick at it. I force down a few bites and then I’m done.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Guilty? I mean… not really sure. I guess everybody feels a little bad about stuff, like not keeping up with things or snapping in traffic or whatever, but I don’t know if I’d call it guilt.\n\nSometimes at work, watching the monitors, my mind kind of drifts and then I think, “Man, I should be paying more attention,” but that’s not really… I don’t know if that’s what you mean. So, no, nothing big that I can point to.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're fat or overweight, even when others say you're not?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t really think that. I mean, I’m not in great shape or anything, I’ve got that middle-aged security guard diet sometimes, vending machine stuff and coffee, but I don’t sit there thinking I’m fat when people say I’m not.\n\nHonestly, I don’t think about my body like that much. My uniform either fits or it doesn’t, and that’s kind of where my mind goes.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything wrong with your appearance?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, I look tired sometimes, but who doesn’t, right? At least nothing I’d call unusual.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten a large amount of food in a short period of time without being able to stop?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I’ve definitely eaten too fast before, especially on nights when you only get a few minutes and you’re trying to watch the cameras at the same time. You sort of inhale a sandwich and then realize you didn’t even enjoy it.\n\nBut not like I couldn’t stop myself. It’s more just bad shift habits, you know? Like vending machine stuff, whatever’s quick. I don’t remember ever feeling out of control with it.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Afraid of gaining weight?     "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n18\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F50.8\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Yeah, um… I came in because I haven’t really been eating much the last few weeks. Like, I’ll get off shift and usually I’ve got certain foods I look forward to, you know, something easy after standing around watching monitors all night—but lately it just kind of sits there. I’ll take a few bites and have to force it down.\n\nIt’s weird because I used to be the guy who could eat whatever after work, especially if the break room had leftovers, but now I just don’t feel hungry. That’s mainly why I figured I should come in.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight over the last few weeks?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really checked the scale, honestly. I mean, my belt maybe feels a little looser, but I don’t know for sure. The bigger thing is I just haven’t been eating much these last few weeks. Stuff I usually like after a shift, I’ll heat it up and then it just kind of sits there, and I’m forcing down a few bites.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in how your clothes fit over the last few weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah, a little maybe—like my belt feels looser, but I’m not totally sure. I haven’t really been checking. I mean, the main thing I noticed is just… I’ll get home after shift and make something I normally like, and it just sits there. I take a few bites and then I’m kind of done. It’s been like that the last few weeks.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in interest in activities you normally enjoy outside of eating?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so. I used to actually look forward to stuff like my morning runs, messing around on guitar, cooking dinner—nothing fancy, just kind of my routine. Lately I keep skipping them because they feel like chores, like I can’t get myself to care enough to start.\n\nI mean, the guitar’s just sitting there in the corner, and I’ll think, “I should pick that up,” then I end up doing nothing. Same with running—used to clear my head a bit, but now it’s like… eh, why bother.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, I think. It’s not always like crying sad or anything, it’s more this heavy, flat feeling I’m carrying around. Like I can be at home with my family, or sitting in the break room at work, and everybody’s talking, and I’m just… not really there.\n\nI don’t know, it’s hard to explain. I’ll look like I’m listening, but inside it’s just kind of dull.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant weight loss?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it significant exactly… I haven’t really been stepping on a scale. My pants feel a little looser, maybe, but the bigger thing is I just haven’t been eating much. Like after work, I’ll make something I normally like, and it just sits there while I pick at it. I force down a few bites and then I’m done.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Guilty? I mean… not really sure. I guess everybody feels a little bad about stuff, like not keeping up with things or snapping in traffic or whatever, but I don’t know if I’d call it guilt.\n\nSometimes at work, watching the monitors, my mind kind of drifts and then I think, “Man, I should be paying more attention,” but that’s not really… I don’t know if that’s what you mean. So, no, nothing big that I can point to.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're fat or overweight, even when others say you're not?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t really think that. I mean, I’m not in great shape or anything, I’ve got that middle-aged security guard diet sometimes, vending machine stuff and coffee, but I don’t sit there thinking I’m fat when people say I’m not.\n\nHonestly, I don’t think about my body like that much. My uniform either fits or it doesn’t, and that’s kind of where my mind goes.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything wrong with your appearance?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, I look tired sometimes, but who doesn’t, right? The lighting in the surveillance room is awful, kind of makes everybody look half-dead on those little reflective screens. Sometimes I’ll catch myself in the bathroom mirror at work and think, “Yeah, you could use a haircut,” or I’ve got stubble or whatever.\n\nBut as far as something wrong with how I look… no, not really. I don’t sit around thinking that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any episodes where you ate a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like, sitting down and eating some huge amount all at once. I mean, I’ll grab junk food on a shift sometimes, like chips or a sandwich from the machine, and then later realize I barely tasted it because I was watching the screens or checking doors.\n\nThere was this one guy I worked with who could demolish a whole pizza during break, and everyone would joke about it, but me… no, I don’t think I have episodes like that. At least nothing I’d call unusual.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten a large amount of food in a short period of time without being able to stop?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I’ve definitely eaten too fast before, especially on nights when you only get a few minutes and you’re trying to watch the cameras at the same time. You sort of inhale a sandwich and then realize you didn’t even enjoy it.\n\nBut not like I couldn’t stop myself. It’s more just bad shift habits, you know? Like vending machine stuff, whatever’s quick. I don’t remember ever feeling out of control with it.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Afraid of gaining weight? Not really… I mean, I notice if my belt’s a little tighter, like anybody would. The uniforms at work don’t exactly hide much, and the pants they issue are never cut right anyway—one size feels like a tent and the next feels like it was made for a teenager.\n\nBut, no, I wouldn’t say I’m scared of gaining weight. I don’t really focus on that much.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you lost control while eating?\n\nPatient: Lost control? No, I don’t think so. I mean, sometimes I’ll eat kind of mindlessly, like if I’m sitting there with a bag of chips and the monitors are on, and then suddenly the bag’s lower than I thought. But that’s more boredom or just not paying attention.\n\nIt’s not like I feel unable to stop, if that’s what you mean. Usually I just eat whatever’s quick and get back to what I’m doing.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost more than a few pounds in the last few weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think I probably have… I haven’t been weighing myself every day or anything, so I can’t give you an exact number. The scale at home is shoved under the sink and I only notice it when I’m looking for cleaner, which is never where it’s supposed to be.\n\nBut I came in partly because the last few weeks I just haven’t had much appetite. Even after a shift, stuff I usually like, I’ll make it or pick it up and then it just kind of sits there. I force down a few bites and that’s about it. So… yeah, I wouldn’t be surprised if I’ve dropped more than a few pounds.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost more than 5% of your body weight in the last month?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s more than 5%. I haven’t really done the math or kept track like that.\n\nI just know the last few weeks I’ve barely had an appetite. Like, after a shift I’ll get something I normally like, and then it just sits there getting cold while I pick at it. I’ll force down a few bites and then I’m done. So I’ve probably lost some, but I couldn’t tell you if it’s that much.\n\nDoctor: Have you been trying to lose weight?\n\nPatient: No, not trying to. I mean, I guess I should probably eat better, everybody says that, and working nights or weird shifts makes it easy to just grab whatever’s around… but I haven’t been dieting or cutting back on purpose.\n\nIt’s more that I came in because the last few weeks I’ve barely had any appetite. Even after a shift, I’ll get something I usually like and it just sits there, and I end up forcing down a few bites.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is anything specifically wrong with the shape of your body?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I haven’t really been focused on that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your lack of appetite is related to your feelings of sadness?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it probably is, at least partly. It’s hard to separate it out, you know? Like I’ll come home after a shift and I’ll have something I usually like, something I used to actually look forward to, and lately it just kind of sits there.\n\nI’ll pick at it, force a few bites, and then I’m done. And then I end up thinking about how much food costs now, which is a whole other thing—everything’s expensive, even the cheap stuff. But yeah, the not eating seems to go along with how down I’ve been feeling these last few weeks.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Yeah, um… I came in because I haven’t really been eating much the last few weeks. Like, I’ll get off shift and usually I’ve got certain foods I look forward to, you know, something easy after standing around watching monitors all night—but lately it just kind of sits there. I’ll take a few bites and have to force it down.\n\nIt’s weird because I used to be the guy who could eat whatever after work, especially if the break room had leftovers, but now I just don’t feel hungry. That’s mainly why I figured I should come in.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight over the last few weeks?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really checked the scale, honestly. I mean, my belt maybe feels a little looser, but I don’t know for sure. The bigger thing is I just haven’t been eating much these last few weeks. Stuff I usually like after a shift, I’ll heat it up and then it just kind of sits there, and I’m forcing down a few bites.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in how your clothes fit over the last few weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah, a little maybe—like my belt feels looser, but I’m not totally sure. I haven’t really been checking. I mean, the main thing I noticed is just… I’ll get home after shift and make something I normally like, and it just sits there. I take a few bites and then I’m kind of done. It’s been like that the last few weeks.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in interest in activities you normally enjoy outside of eating?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so. I used to actually look forward to stuff like my morning runs, messing around on guitar, cooking dinner—nothing fancy, just kind of my routine. Lately I keep skipping them because they feel like chores, like I can’t get myself to care enough to start.\n\nI mean, the guitar’s just sitting there in the corner, and I’ll think, “I should pick that up,” then I end up doing nothing. Same with running—used to clear my head a bit, but now it’s like… eh, why bother.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, I think. It’s not always like crying sad or anything, it’s more this heavy, flat feeling I’m carrying around. Like I can be at home with my family, or sitting in the break room at work, and everybody’s talking, and I’m just… not really there.\n\nI don’t know, it’s hard to explain. I’ll look like I’m listening, but inside it’s just kind of dull.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant weight loss?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it significant exactly… I haven’t really been stepping on a scale. My pants feel a little looser, maybe, but the bigger thing is I just haven’t been eating much. Like after work, I’ll make something I normally like, and it just sits there while I pick at it. I force down a few bites and then I’m done.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Guilty? I mean… not really sure. I guess everybody feels a little bad about stuff, like not keeping up with things or snapping in traffic or whatever, but I don’t know if I’d call it guilt.\n\nSometimes at work, watching the monitors, my mind kind of drifts and then I think, “Man, I should be paying more attention,” but that’s not really… I don’t know if that’s what you mean. So, no, nothing big that I can point to.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're fat or overweight, even when others say you're not?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t really think that. I mean, I’m not in great shape or anything, I’ve got that middle-aged security guard diet sometimes, vending machine stuff and coffee, but I don’t sit there thinking I’m fat when people say I’m not.\n\nHonestly, I don’t think about my body like that much. My uniform either fits or it doesn’t, and that’s kind of where my mind goes.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything wrong with your appearance?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, I look tired sometimes, but who doesn’t, right? The lighting in the surveillance room is awful, kind of makes everybody look half-dead on those little reflective screens. Sometimes I’ll catch myself in the bathroom mirror at work and think, “Yeah, you could use a haircut,” or I’ve got stubble or whatever.\n\nBut as far as something wrong with how I look… no, not really. I don’t sit around thinking that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any episodes where you ate a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like, sitting down and eating some huge amount all at once. I mean, I’ll grab junk food on a shift sometimes, like chips or a sandwich from the machine, and then later realize I barely tasted it because I was watching the screens or checking doors.\n\nThere was this one guy I worked with who could demolish a whole pizza during break, and everyone would joke about it, but me… no, I don’t think I have episodes like that. At least nothing I’d call unusual.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten a large amount of food in a short period of time without being able to stop?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I’ve definitely eaten too fast before, especially on nights when you only get a few minutes and you’re trying to watch the cameras at the same time. You sort of inhale a sandwich and then realize you didn’t even enjoy it.\n\nBut not like I couldn’t stop myself. It’s more just bad shift habits, you know? Like vending machine stuff, whatever’s quick. I don’t remember ever feeling out of control with it.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Afraid of gaining weight? Not really… I mean, I notice if my belt’s a little tighter, like anybody would. The uniforms at work don’t exactly hide much, and the pants they issue are never cut right anyway—one size feels like a tent and the next feels like it was made for a teenager.\n\nBut, no, I wouldn’t say I’m scared of gaining weight. I don’t really focus on that much.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you lost control while eating?\n\nPatient: Lost control? No, I don’t think so. I mean, sometimes I’ll eat kind of mindlessly, like if I’m sitting there with a bag of chips and the monitors are on, and then suddenly the bag’s lower than I thought. But that’s more boredom or just not paying attention.\n\nIt’s not like I feel unable to stop, if that’s what you mean. Usually I just eat whatever’s quick and get back to what I’m doing.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost more than a few pounds in the last few weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think I probably have… I haven’t been weighing myself every day or anything, so I can’t give you an exact number. The scale at home is shoved under the sink and I only notice it when I’m looking for cleaner, which is never where it’s supposed to be.\n\nBut I came in partly because the last few weeks I just haven’t had much appetite. Even after a shift, stuff I usually like, I’ll make it or pick it up and then it just kind of sits there. I force down a few bites and that’s about it. So… yeah, I wouldn’t be surprised if I’ve dropped more than a few pounds.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost more than 5% of your body weight in the last month?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s more than 5%. I haven’t really done the math or kept track like that.\n\nI just know the last few weeks I’ve barely had an appetite. Like, after a shift I’ll get something I normally like, and then it just sits there getting cold while I pick at it. I’ll force down a few bites and then I’m done. So I’ve probably lost some, but I couldn’t tell you if it’s that much.\n\nDoctor: Have you been trying to lose weight?\n\nPatient: No, not trying to. I mean, I guess I should probably eat better, everybody says that, and working nights or weird shifts makes it easy to just grab whatever’s around… but I haven’t been dieting or cutting back on purpose.\n\nIt’s more that I came in because the last few weeks I’ve barely had any appetite. Even after a shift, I’ll get something I usually like and it just sits there, and I end up forcing down a few bites.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is anything specifically wrong with the shape of your body?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. The uniforms at work don’t exactly hide much, and the pants they issue are never cut right anyway—one size feels like a tent and the next feels like it was made for a teenager.\n\nBut, no, I wouldn’t say I’m scared of gaining weight. I don’t really focus on that much.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you lost control while eating?\n\nPatient: Lost control? No, I don’t think so. I mean, sometimes I’ll eat kind of mindlessly, like if I’m sitting there with a bag of chips and the monitors are on, and then suddenly the bag’s lower than I thought. But that’s more boredom or just not paying attention.\n\nIt’s not like I feel unable to stop, if that’s what you mean. Usually I just eat whatever’s quick and get back to what I’m doing.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost more than a few pounds in the last few weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think I probably have… I haven’t been weighing myself every day or anything, so I can’t give you an exact number. The scale at home is shoved under the sink and I only notice it when I’m looking for cleaner, which is never where it’s supposed to be.\n\nBut I came in partly because the last few weeks I just haven’t had much appetite. Like, after a shift I’ll get something I normally like, and then it just sits there getting cold while I pick at it. I’ll force down a few bites and then I’m done. So I’ve probably lost some, but I couldn’t tell you if it’s that much.\n\nDoctor: Have you been trying to lose weight?\n\nPatient: No, not trying to. I mean, I guess I should probably eat better, everybody says that, and working nights or weird shifts makes it easy to just grab whatever’s around… but I haven’t been dieting or cutting back on purpose.\n\nIt’s more that I came in because the last few weeks I’ve barely had any appetite. Even after a shift, I’ll get something I usually like and it just sits there, and I end up forcing down a few bites.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is anything specifically wrong with the shape of your body?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I haven’t really been focused on that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your lack of appetite is related to your feelings of sadness?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it probably is, at least partly. And then I end up thinking about how much food costs now, which is a whole other thing—everything’s expensive, even the cheap stuff.
